## Vision and users

Elsa 4 is the workflow engine and BPMN engine for .NET, with a foundation that can later support a first-class ETL engine. Developers author and extend executable behavior; operators recover and inspect executions; process participants complete human work; integration authors connect systems. They share a coherent platform without forcing every semantic model into one graph or interpreter.

Optimize for correctness, architectural fit, clear responsibility and extensibility. Existing frameworks and custom implementations are alternatives to evaluate, not commitments justified by familiarity or AI-assisted implementation cost.

## Agreed direction and boundaries

1. One durable execution foundation; multiple semantic engines and extensible composition components.
2. Flowchart owns joins, Sequence owns ordering, StateMachine owns transitions. Shared runtime mechanisms do not reinterpret those rules or infer parent completion merely because child jobs ended.
3. A workflow/process instance is the virtual-actor coordination boundary. Activities and composites do not become individual actors. A pipeline execution may have an addressable coordinator; independently leased partition workers need not be actors.
4. Distinguish semantic scope, state ownership, actor activation and computational work. One orchestration owner can coordinate many concurrent workers.
5. Actor hosting is pluggable. Preserve ADR 0078: one membership view, capability-aware placement, authoritative commit fencing and durable owed-work tracking. Orleans is the first adapter experiment, not a selected default.
6. Background activity execution and ETL partitions should share only demonstrated durable dispatch/recovery mechanisms. Activity authoring remains independent of actor-framework APIs. Offloading does not change Sequence/Flowchart semantics.
7. Activities eligible for offloading need explicit invocation identity, pinned implementation, isolated inputs/context and correlated outputs/effects. Live mutable coordinator context must not escape to workers. Business errors, technical failures and incidents remain distinct.
8. BPMN is first-class. Direct BPMN hosting and the BpmnProcess embedding adapter use the same semantics implementation. Preserve existing frozen contracts and adopt #2735's library work rather than creating a second token engine.
9. ETL target: native .NET; finite batches and continuous event integration; distributed partitions with a local transform chain; bounded batches/backpressure; mappings, filtering, validation, routing and lookups. Files, HTTP, SQL and RabbitMQ are initial connector candidates. Defer cross-worker individual stages, windows/stream joins and general analytics.
10. At-least-once execution with explicit idempotency and external-effect recovery. No universal exactly-once side-effect claim.
11. Design/runtime separation, immutable pinned artifacts, tenant/authority propagation, versioned state, recovery and observable operational outcomes are requirements.
12. Human tasks, incident tools, alterations, migration and analytics are surrounding modules. Identify their foundation contracts now; do not implement the whole product in this program's evidence milestone.
13. Interactive HTML follows evidence. It communicates the architecture and its limits; visual polish is not proof.

These are owner-approved directions from this conversation, not newly ratified public interfaces or replacements for accepted ADRs. Where the evidence implies changing an accepted contract, obtain a reviewed ADR amendment before production work.

## Primary journeys and success criteria

- A native workflow schedules independent background activities concurrently, preserves sequential composition, persists outcomes and resumes after a crash without losing or repeating logical continuation.
- A BPMN process runs directly and through BpmnProcess with equivalent normalized semantic traces: fork/join, restart, human wait, interrupting timer, late result, worker incident and a narrowly validated alteration.
- A workflow or BPMN service task invokes a distributed pipeline. Partitions progress independently; a worker dies after external delivery but before checkpoint; recovery uses destination idempotency and refuses stale progress. Parent success, fault and cancellation map explicitly.
- The built-in host satisfies its declared conformance requirements without changing definitions or activity code. Real third-party provider qualification remains tracked but owner-deferred under the 20 October reprioritization; it is not passed, and Proto.Actor has not been selected.
- A separate package adds a fourth semantic model without new model-specific branches in the common substrate; unsupported capabilities are refused clearly.
- An operator can inspect durable work, incidents and causal history through supported boundaries. A browser reader can explore the evidence-backed architecture through the later HTML artifact.

## Source evidence and its limits

The existing report `docs/reports/unified-execution-three-case-proof.md` and prototype at `tools/experiments/UnifiedExecution.Prototype/` are historical evidence at local commit `4cc63596d`: 115 assertions, 68 worker processes and three negative mutations, with 622 architecture tests and map checks reported. These are prior results, not newly run gates or multi-host proof. Lease time was logical, not a real distributed clock experiment. The prototype does not prove BPMN conformance, framework substitution, general commit replay, complete cancellation or plugin registration.

The local checkout predates the source-layout consolidation visible in live issue #2501 (Essentials/, Extensions/, Studio/). Before implementation refresh upstream refs, current paths, ownership, claims and exact-head checks. Never copy stale source paths into a new implementation task without resolving them.

Relevant sources: local actor/placement/checkpoint implementations; Elsa 3 background middleware and invoker inspected at be5bdae50; actual Bpmn.Semantics integration owned by #2735; ADRs 0031, 0063, 0073 and 0078. Camunda worker/task/modification/migration documentation informs capability boundaries, not a compatibility promise.

Later feature tasks are progressively elaborated; no speculative production leaf backlog is ready today.

## Repository and existing-program ownership

- `elsa-workflows/elsa-foundation` owns this program's coordination and future shared execution work. Resolve current monorepo paths under #2501 before implementation; optional integrations follow the approved extension layout.
- `valence-works/bpmn` owns reusable BPMN semantics. Foundation #2735 owns its Elsa 4 adoption/parity. Link upstream dependencies; do not duplicate or reparent existing work.
- Existing BPMN Engine, Runtime Execution Seam, Runtime Alterations and Runtime Execution Evidence buckets retain their current scopes. In particular Execution Evidence is a product capability, not this research ledger.
- Foundation #2771 retains Elsa 3/4 parity ownership; #2747 incident retry and #1307 pump concurrency are related findings to reconcile, not automatically absorbed tasks.
- #2501 and #1970 retain repository/release topology. This program does not rename repositories or assume local historical paths are current.

## Evidence requirements

Every witness records exact source/library/provider versions, commands, fixtures, observable outcomes, negative controls, raw durable artifacts where safe, untested boundaries and an independent review. Test duplicate and conflicting receipts; crash before dispatch and after external effect; expiry and stale writers; cancellation versus late success; early versus late completion; fault versus business error; tenant/authority isolation and incompatible version placement where the witness crosses those boundaries. Logical-clock and single-machine evidence must remain labelled as such; the provider witness must include real multi-process cluster behavior and state its network-failure coverage.

Do not use scheduler work-count, issue closure or an animation as a success proxy. No throughput claims or reinstatement of retired benchmark gates. Production changes later use the normal Speckit/constitution/e2e and review gates for the affected scope.

## Interactive HTML presentation brief

Deliver after M2 evidence review, as a self-contained HTML artifact with a usable offline fallback. Provide overview, semantic composition, execution-instance/actor, worker/dataflow and recovery views. Support pan/zoom, drill-down, breadcrumbs, a legend and accessible keyboard navigation. Scenario controls cover normal execution, crash/reclaim, duplicate result and cancellation races. Distinguish a visualization/simulation from a live engine trace. Link every asserted guarantee to its evidence and mark proposed/unsupported behavior. Show workflow instance as the actor boundary, not every node; show ETL records bypassing the coordinator. Include source links, evidence revision/date, reduced-motion support and a concise presenter path. Use 3D only if it improves comprehension. Do not build or publish it before the evidence gate.

## Continuity protocol

GitHub issues are the authoritative progress/ownership record. This document owns product intent and staged acceptance; accepted ADRs own architecture gates; reports own findings; specs own approved work-unit details. The program-goal file is the repository entrypoint, not a competing task board.

On every resume: read the program issue and latest checkpoint; read this plan and the existing proof report; refresh all relevant issue/PR/dependency/claim state; resolve current source topology; identify the single active leaf or next eligible leaf. Read the leaf acceptance criteria and required evidence before touching code. Never infer that a closed issue, an old green check or a prior chat means current proof passed.

At each material transition: update the leaf issue with exact branch/commit, evidence, next action and blocker; reconcile parent acceptance and ready state; update the program's compact status table. Release issue claims explicitly. Checkpoint before context compaction or handoff. One active leaf by default; independent research can be delegated read-only. Keep future production features out of the worker queue until their requirements gate passes.

A checkpoint contains: current milestone; active/next issue; branch/worktree and exact SHA; last verified evidence; blockers and decision owners; next concrete command/action; things not yet proved. Durable evidence must be committed or embedded/uploaded to GitHub, not exist only in /tmp or chat. If a required document is only local, attach its content to the program issue until a normal documentation PR makes it available remotely.

## Native proof delivery — 2026-10-17

Initial delivery was in draft PR #2915 at `539df7cce7a9fcf2c909fda703f53b46408a927e`. [Initial acceptance record and reproducible commands](https://github.com/elsa-workflows/elsa-foundation/blob/539df7cce7a9fcf2c909fda703f53b46408a927e/specs/241-native-durable-offload/quickstart.md). The results below describe that original local proof. Subsequent CI and review corrections culminated in approval of `b15dfc2688df06bcc1a167af6b186146d7648bb0` and merge as `6bcce9fdcc291684a396b80d6c2f7ce3f3e25e53` on 18 October 2026. [Merge and verification receipt](https://github.com/elsa-workflows/elsa-foundation/pull/2915#issuecomment-6244837243). Post-merge verification is complete on owner-accepted descendant `75bb7b1993b2a274ed58ae2bc9dcf3cb137b11ab`: backend CI, Studio, CodeQL and maps passed. Original merge-commit CI/CodeQL remain cancelled, not passed. [Final evidence receipt](https://github.com/elsa-workflows/elsa-foundation/pull/2915#issuecomment-6247136655). Deployment remains outside this task. The historical receipt timeout remains causally unexplained.

The rebuilt Workbench passes all three real-process cases: both background completion orders and REST cancellation across restart. Recovery preserves invocation/attempt identities, repairs a committed receipt without rerunning its body, and advances the final native step once. Full affected suites pass: activity runtime 485, publishing 776, workflow runtime 2,338, Sequence 22, Flowchart 121, Runtime EF 969, and architecture 576. Final maps and solution-filter checks pass. Ten production-source negative controls fail when their safeguard is removed and pass after restoration.

The evidence records review fixes for publication placement, lease-renewal response races, coalesced first admission, deletion fencing and in-memory parity. Physical bodies remain at least once. Process recovery is exercised with concurrent bodies in one host at a time over SQLite; simultaneous multi-host placement, other actor providers, and live recovery on other database engines remain separate work. Broader replay issue #2232 and baseline main CI #2293 are not declared resolved by this proof.

## Historical three-case proof snapshot

Preserved here so the report survives loss of the research checkout. These are previously reported results at local commit `4cc63596d`, not tests rerun during program setup. The executable source remains in that local commit until #2895 verifies or creates its remote archive. The following report retains its historical paths and disposition; Program #2892 now governs sequencing.

# Unified execution foundation: three-case architecture proof

Date: 2026-10-17. Base inspected: `e4a699879791fb7eb2bc1c6874f772f4a7fe5355`.
State: **research evidence; not an accepted production architecture or implementation spec**.
Program route: **none/free-flow**. This experiment informs the existing
[BPMN Engine](../program-goals/bpmn-engine.md) and [Runtime Execution Seam](../program-goals/runtime-execution-seam.md)
programs without claiming or changing their delivery units.

## Question and result

Can process orchestration and partitioned data processing share durable execution mechanisms without
forcing one computational model onto the other?

**The three bounded cases support that hypothesis. They do not yet justify a universal execution
kernel.** A small executable demonstrates the same fenced, atomic checkpoint path for process
continuations and independent data partitions, including parent/child composition. It does not run
today's Elsa runtime, and it does not prove that the proposed boundary hosts BPMN. The user added
BPMN as a required semantic family during this experiment; that adds a fourth gate before any
generalization or production extraction.

The [throwaway .NET prototype](../../tools/experiments/UnifiedExecution.Prototype/README.md) uses the
repository's .NET 10 target and pinned Microsoft.Data.Sqlite package. It is a nonpackable solution
tool with no production consumers, uses no production data, and introduces no Elsa public API or
schema migration. Solution registration preserves the repository's lock-file ownership guard.
Its CLI state inspector is shared tooling evidence, **not** a visual-designer integration proof.

## What was exercised

| Witness | Deliberate disturbance | Required observation |
|---|---|---|
| Approval workflow | Waiting worker exits; approval is durably accepted before another worker starts; duplicate signal is delivered | Approved and rejected branches both complete once; malformed approval cannot authorize work |
| Distributed partition pipeline | Two OS workers hold distinct leases concurrently; one is held while the other progresses | Different partitions advance independently through one coordination store |
| Pipeline recovery | Worker exits after its destination transaction commits, before its coordinator checkpoint | Recovery rereads the batch; 20 record delivery attempts produce 18 distinct transformed records, sum 1710 |
| Stale/expired ownership | Logical lease expires; a successor commits; old worker is then released | Old checkpoint is refused; expired ownership is also refused without a successor |
| Bounded pull | Destination is held before writing | Worker holds one two-record batch; no subsequent batch is read or checkpointed |
| Workflow embeds pipeline | Child worker dies after destination delivery | Child recovers independently; parent completes with a dataset reference rather than row payloads |
| Completion ordering | Child finishes before or after the parent registers its wait | Neither ordering loses completion; a duplicate outcome cannot repeat the parent continuation |
| Artifact identity | Changed pipeline multiplier is published while an older execution exists | Existing execution continues with the original content-addressed artifact |
| Conflicting replay | Reuse a start/signal/output identity with different data | Conflicting parent, seed, signal and sink output are rejected |

Each `worker` invocation is a fresh OS process. Two workers overlap at an explicit barrier; this is
not merely two objects called sequentially. Recovery does not depend on an in-memory continuation.
The sink is a **different SQLite database** with an independent transaction, deliberately exposing
the external-effect/checkpoint gap. Stable record identities and sink idempotency close that gap
for this example. Coordinator fencing alone does not prevent duplicate external attempts.

Lease time is a manually advanced logical clock. This tests ownership transitions without timing
assertions; it does not prove wall-clock behavior, distributed clock tolerance or lease heartbeats.

## Candidate shared boundary

The executable's `Store` does not inspect process phases, pipeline cursors or artifact kinds.
Both strategies use its artifact lookup, execution/job identities, atomic claims, fencing,
checkpoints, durable named signals, child creation and completion publication. `Engine` is the
composition root that selects a strategy from an artifact kind. It also prepares a child through
the selected strategy; the parent strategy never invokes the dataflow strategy directly.

| Potentially common mechanism | Semantics that remain owned by the execution model |
|---|---|
| Artifact identity and pinned execution binding | Definition language, validation and compilation |
| Work-unit claims and fencing | What a work unit means and where its ownership boundary lies |
| Atomic durable transition | Process phase, pipeline cursor, BPMN token/scope state |
| Durable event receipt and wake-up | Approval decisions, BPMN correlation/races, source acknowledgements |
| Child execution identity and outcome delivery | Workflow continuation, BPMN task/subprocess/call semantics |
| Shared observation envelope | Process history, partition progress, BPMN tokens and scopes |

In particular, pipelines do not create an activity execution per record. The three-partition
pipeline uses three durable jobs and nine successful batch checkpoints. The approval workflow
uses one continuation job. They share durability, not scheduling granularity or payload state.

## What the experiment does not settle

The current toy substrate deliberately makes simplifying choices. These are findings to challenge,
not proposed public contracts:

1. **Completion authority:** the store equates all jobs completed with execution completion. That
   fits these cases, but is not a sufficient universal rule for BPMN scopes, active subscriptions,
   event subprocesses or compensation. A semantic engine must decide its terminal verdict and
   obligations; the substrate validates and commits it.
2. **Ownership granularity:** jobs are independently fenced. A BPMN scope often needs one owner
   to serialize token and event decisions. Pipeline partitions need independent owners. The
   experiment does not yet prove configurable ownership scopes spanning several work items.
3. **Event semantics:** signals here are retained, immutable, named one-shot receipts. This is not
   a message correlation engine, a repeated event subscription model or a BPMN event-race contract.
4. **Effect protocol:** sink deduplication is proven for deterministic keyed rows. General external
   effects, nondeterministic transforms, uncertain HTTP responses and transactional source/sink
   combinations remain connector-specific. There is no universal exactly-once claim.
5. **Commit replay:** a consumed lease is refused and a new worker loads committed state. Exact
   commit-response replay, commit fingerprints and conflicting duplicate commit detection are not
   implemented; today's Elsa checkpoint machinery already has stronger precedents here.
6. **Lifecycle:** cancellation, fault propagation, compensation, tenant/authority propagation,
   retention, schema evolution and continuous subscriptions are outside this executable proof.
7. **Atomic start failure:** parent checkpoint and child insertion use one transaction in source,
   but the driver does not kill a process inside that transaction or during commit acknowledgement.
   Do not claim that crash boundary was independently exercised.

No throughput or latency benchmark, performance budget or timing gate was introduced.

## BPMN: engine first, activity as an adapter

Current source already has the right conceptual distinction:

- `BpmnProcess` delegates start/completion/fault/notification callbacks to `BpmnExecutionEngine`.
- `BpmnExecutionState` owns token, event-race, loop, compensation and transaction state.
- `BpmnScheduler` applies decisions through Elsa child-execution capabilities.
- `BpmnStatePersister` stages typed, versioned private state through the existing runtime.

See [BpmnProcess](../../src/essentials/Activities/Bpmn/Activities/BpmnProcess.cs),
[BPMN extension points](../../src/essentials/Activities/Bpmn/EXTENSION_POINTS.md) and the
[current engine documentation](../../src/essentials/Activities/Bpmn/README.md).

The live [Foundation issue #2735](https://github.com/elsa-workflows/elsa-foundation/issues/2735)
already owns migration to `Bpmn.Semantics`, parity analysis and retirement of the duplicate in-repo
semantics core. Its current comments also contain parity findings and upstream dependencies.
This experiment neither claims that issue nor changes its timing, bindings or frozen contracts.

**Revised direction:** offer native BPMN artifact execution and retain `BpmnProcess` as an
embedding/compatibility adapter. Both must use the same BPMN semantics implementation. Do not make
a generic flowchart or dataflow interpreter reinterpret BPMN token rules. Do not replace the
existing BPMN activity merely because the new platform calls semantic runtimes “engines”.

A BPMN service-task binding may invoke an Elsa pipeline. That is an Elsa integration binding,
not a claim that arbitrary ETL graphs are standard BPMN call activities. Conversely, a workflow
may embed a BPMN process through the adapter. Errors, cancellation and completion at those
boundaries need explicit mappings; a generic successful child-result string is insufficient.

## Fourth witness and decision gate

Before promoting shared contracts, host the **actual `Bpmn.Semantics` implementation** behind a
candidate runtime adapter. This is the next bounded architecture unit, not a new BPMN interpreter.

1. Pin the library version selected by the #2735 parity work. Preserve its model and persisted
   semantic state; upstream missing semantics rather than duplicating them in Elsa.
2. Execute a BPMN parallel fork/join with two bound tasks, one waiting durably. Restart between
   branch completions; require the join to fire once, only when both required tokens arrive.
3. Exercise an interrupting boundary event and a late completion from the cancelled task. Require
   correct scope teardown and refusal of the late token. Test both event arrival orders.
4. Run that process both as a native BPMN artifact and through the existing activity adapter.
   Compare semantic trace and outcome, allowing only host-specific execution identifiers to differ.
5. Bind one BPMN service task to the proven pipeline. Crash/recover the pipeline; require exactly
   one logical BPMN task completion and an explicit data-reference result. Add child fault and
   cancellation outcomes before accepting the cross-engine lifecycle contract.
6. Challenge the prototype's completion authority, ownership scope and signal model. Extract only
   the smallest mechanisms that survive all four witnesses. Keep BPMN token/scope/compensation
   decisions out of the shared infrastructure.

The fourth witness establishes hosting suitability, **not full BPMN conformance**. A proper Elsa 4
BPMN release additionally requires an explicit supported execution profile and evidence matrix
against the [OMG BPMN 2.0.2 specification](https://www.omg.org/spec/BPMN/2.0.2/About-BPMN): executable
constructs and stated cuts, token/join behavior, events and correlation, scope cancellation,
subprocess/call behavior, transactions/compensation, multi-instance behavior, and XML/BPMNDI
interchange. Unsupported semantics must be diagnosed rather than silently approximated.

Retain the #2735 parity/fingerprint and Studio wire-compatibility gates. This report does not
ratify provisional constitution sections or authorize changing existing activity contracts.

## Reproduction and evidence

Run the command in the prototype README. Each run prints its scratch directory, a concise verdict,
and an inspection command. `evidence.json` retains the executed assertion count, process count,
execution records and checkpoint audit. The malformed-input example intentionally remains
nonterminal because an incident-resolution UI is outside the experiment.

The initial reviewed execution passed **115 assertions using 68 fresh worker processes**.
After review fixes, all three source mutations were applied separately, built, run and restored:

| Negative control | Observed failure |
|---|---|
| Bypass checkpoint fencing | Old owner checkpoint assertion fails |
| Remove destination key uniqueness | Replayed batch produces duplicate rows; expected output assertion fails |
| Ignore already-received signals when registering a wait | Early child completion is lost; parent continuation assertion fails |

Each mutated run exited 1 for its expected failed invariant. The restored executable again passed
**115 assertions / 68 fresh worker processes**. A piped interactive approval journey also completed
with `approved`, and the generated-maps check reported that committed maps still describe the tree.
Final scratch evidence: `elsa-unified-PROTOTYPE-c22cb55d54f8493aa693698b7d93ffeb` beneath the machine's
temporary directory; negative-control logs are `elsa-unified-proof-mutation-*.log` there. These are
local ephemeral evidence, not committed fixtures; rerunning creates a new independent directory.

The architecture suite initially reported 23 failures with only its own dependency graph restored.
Focused diagnostics confirmed missing full-solution Release/Debug and host restore assets. Running
`bash tools/architecture/restore-ci-project-graph.sh -p:RestoreLockedMode=true --verbosity quiet`
supplied those prerequisites without changing lock files. The next run exposed one real experiment
integration mistake: its lock file had no solution project. The tool was then registered in
`Elsa.Server.slnx`; no guard was weakened or bypassed. The final gate result is recorded below.

Final verification:

- `dotnet test tests/essentials/Architecture/Elsa.Architecture.Tests.csproj --no-build --no-restore --verbosity quiet`:
  **622 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped** after the prerequisites and solution registration above.
- Generated maps: **pass**, including a final check after solution registration; no map changed.
- Independent prototype review: four initial correctness findings fixed (approval validation,
  conflicting starts, invalid batch settings, conflicting sink replay); final source review found
  no further concrete defect. Its report-wording corrections were incorporated.
- `git diff --cached --check`: **pass**. No production runtime suite or browser journey is claimed;
  production source and UI were not modified. No remote CI or merge gate result is claimed.

## Disposition

Keep the prototype temporarily for architectural review; delete it after the decision is accepted
or superseded. Keep this report as the durable finding. Do not extract a production kernel from
this schema. The immediate next unit is the real BPMN adapter witness, coordinated with #2735,
followed by a reviewed architecture/specification decision covering all semantic families.

--- comments 6249319967 https://github.com/elsa-workflows/elsa-foundation/issues/2892#issuecomment-6249319967 2026-10-18T11:23:15Z ---

## Approved delivery continuation — 18 October 2026

Sipke approved the complete continuation plan and instructed: “Implement the proposed plan.” This extends the completed native slice into end-to-end delivery of Program #2892. The issue hierarchy remains authoritative, with one active implementation leaf and one integration lane.

### Ratified product and architecture direction

- Shared durable execution infrastructure with separate model semantics and component-owned composition. Native/BPMN production integrations must prove the neutral contract before its public surface is treated as stable. Existing EF checkpoint/outbox authority remains authoritative.
- Built-in hosting remains the default; qualify optional Orleans hosting with the same conformance suite. A separately packaged rule model proves extension without kernel model-name branches. Agent composition does not count as the fourth model.
- First-class direct and embedded BPMN use one released Bpmn.Semantics interpreter. Preserve #2735 ownership and parity requirements. BPMN was reactivated through #2763; stale parked wording is not the current owner direction. Version 0.3.1 excludes the breaking fault-path fix, so release/parity verification remains a prerequisite.
- BPMN foundation APIs include task claim/completion, external work, correlation, timers, incidents/retry, cancellation and inspection. Arbitrary token alteration, live-instance migration and a full operator console are explicitly deferred. Earlier research criteria demanding arbitrary alteration must be recorded as deferred by this decision, never marked as demonstrated.
- ETL includes all four connector families: CSV/JSON Lines files; paginated JSON HTTP ingestion and JSON delivery; PostgreSQL and SQL Server snapshot/watermark reads and transactional keyed writes; RabbitMQ JSON ingestion/delivery with manual acknowledgements and publisher confirms. Log CDC, distributed stage shuffle, windows/stream joins and general analytics are deferred.
- ETL owns partition cursors, local transform chains, mapping/filtering/validation/routing/lookups, bounded batches, byte limits and durable admission budgets. No per-record workflow execution or physical-memory guarantee inferred from lease expiry.
- Production agent collaboration is in scope through existing Elsa agent infrastructure and native composition: typed message routes/rounds, scoped memory/knowledge/tool bindings, durably accepted decisions, exact authorized tool approvals, budgets before dispatch, cancellation and recovery. Deterministic and provider-protocol tests are the completion gate; live model smoke tests are opt-in and unexecuted live behavior will not be claimed.
- Integrated Studio authoring/inspection and the existing HTML presentation are part of delivery. Complete offline/direct-file and accessibility verification through an authorized browser harness.

### Ordered delivery

1. Reconcile the hierarchy, decisions and durable repository entrypoint; preserve completed evidence and fix stale status metadata.
2. Specify and implement the smallest shared production lifecycle boundary, preserving native compatibility and proving real native/BPMN integration.
3. Complete owner-aligned BPMN library adoption and operational foundation; progress independent work while released-library prerequisites are outstanding.
4. Qualify built-in/Orleans hosting and an independently packaged fourth model.
5. Deliver ETL runtime, each connector's replay/checkpoint/acknowledgement guarantees, workflow/BPMN invocation and Studio experience.
6. Deliver native agent composition, existing provider adapters, durable approval/resource/budget behavior and Studio experience.
7. Update and verify the presentation; demonstrate all supported journeys and reconcile each parent acceptance before closure.

Every implementation leaf uses scoped claims, isolated worktrees, specifications, meaningful negative controls, affected unit/integration/REST/browser checks, exact-head review, CI/maps and post-merge verification. Published definitions and saved executions remain compatible; persisted schemas are versioned. Rollback stops new capability admission while retaining capable readers/workers for accepted instances. External effects remain at least once unless a connector supplies a stronger verified contract.

Completion means integrated working capabilities on main with reproducible acceptance evidence. It does not authorize manual package publication, deployment, permission/workflow changes, live model spending, or takeover of another active owner's claim. Program #2892 remains in progress. #2914/#2915 remain complete with their accepted descendant evidence and preserved exact-merge cancellations; their historical timeout is still causally unexplained. No new monitoring automation is created by this continuation.

--- comments 6279240972 https://github.com/elsa-workflows/elsa-foundation/issues/2892#issuecomment-6279240972 2026-10-19T23:01:38Z ---

Next hosting slice prepared — inactive until #3056 is verified

The following draft incorporates root and independent source review. It records the already-authorized successor without opening or claiming another implementation leaf. #3056 remains the sole active leaf while PR #3087 completes its gates. Orleans membership/API feasibility remains an investigation requirement; no adapter qualification or settled implementation design is claimed.

<details>
<summary>Reviewed draft objective, constraints and acceptance</summary>

# Task: Add an optional native Orleans adapter and qualify it against the shared suite

**Draft only.** Do not activate or claim until #3056 is merged and post-merge verification is complete. The latest queue record keeps #3056 as the sole active leaf; PR #3087 hosted exact-head checks and resulting-main verification were still pending ([status](https://github.com/elsa-workflows/elsa-foundation/issues/3056#issuecomment-6279000285)). Refresh the shared queue, main-red issues, claims and matching PRs before activation.

**Parent:** [#2905](https://github.com/elsa-workflows/elsa-foundation/issues/2905), under [program #2892](https://github.com/elsa-workflows/elsa-foundation/issues/2892). Cross-link [#2899](https://github.com/elsa-workflows/elsa-foundation/issues/2899) and the bounded research in [#2900](https://github.com/elsa-workflows/elsa-foundation/issues/2900). The owner-authorized scope is recorded in [#2892 comment 6249319967](https://github.com/elsa-workflows/elsa-foundation/issues/2892#issuecomment-6249319967); #2900 is research, not production acceptance. #3056 supplies the shared native fixture after verified delivery. BPMN remains owned by [#2735](https://github.com/elsa-workflows/elsa-foundation/issues/2735).

## Objective

Add a separately packaged, opt-in Orleans provider for native workflow execution and qualify it using the same applicable scenarios as the built-in host. Keep built-in hosting as the default, preserve definitions and native semantics, and leave checkpoint fencing and durable owed-work delivery authoritative in EF. Do not add a second semantic engine or expose Orleans APIs to workflows/activities.

## Constraints and design gate

- [ADR 0078](https://github.com/elsa-workflows/elsa-foundation/blob/b4d576e624ee8854822171dd3662ae02b4145727/docs/adr/0078-workflow-executions-are-virtual-actors-and-cluster-membership-is-a-foundation-contract.md#L102-L135) requires one membership authority, commit-time fencing independent of routing, capability queries through membership, and a durable queue as the record of owed work.
- [IClusterMembership](https://github.com/elsa-workflows/elsa-foundation/blob/b4d576e624ee8854822171dd3662ae02b4145727/Essentials/src/Cluster/Core/Contracts/IClusterMembership.cs#L18-L58) requires one provider, a complete authoritative `Fresh` read or an exception, and report publication visible to every later fresh read. Establish whether the currently supported Orleans membership APIs can also provide capability reports, incarnation/lapse, draining and those read-visibility semantics. Do not assume cached/gossip membership is equivalent, or compose EF membership beside Orleans.
- [Execution envelopes and activation/passivation requests](https://github.com/elsa-workflows/elsa-foundation/blob/b4d576e624ee8854822171dd3662ae02b4145727/Essentials/src/Workflows/Runtime/Core/Models/WorkflowExecutionActorModels.cs#L23-L66) carry `WorkflowExecutionPartition`; activation/passivation do too ([activation](https://github.com/elsa-workflows/elsa-foundation/blob/b4d576e624ee8854822171dd3662ae02b4145727/Essentials/src/Workflows/Runtime/Core/Models/WorkflowExecutionActorModels.cs#L141-L171), [passivation](https://github.com/elsa-workflows/elsa-foundation/blob/b4d576e624ee8854822171dd3662ae02b4145727/Essentials/src/Workflows/Runtime/Core/Models/WorkflowExecutionActorModels.cs#L212-L238)). Preserve `(partition, workflow execution ID)` in actor addressing.
- [The actor-provider contract](https://github.com/elsa-workflows/elsa-foundation/blob/b4d576e624ee8854822171dd3662ae02b4145727/Essentials/src/Workflows/Runtime/Core/Contracts/IWorkflowExecutionActorProvider.cs#L6-L24) owns the serialized mailbox but leaves commit fencing downstream. The scheduler queue requires idempotent admission by `(WorkflowExecutionId, WorkItemId)` ([IWorkflowSchedulerWorkQueue](https://github.com/elsa-workflows/elsa-foundation/blob/b4d576e624ee8854822171dd3662ae02b4145727/Essentials/src/Workflows/Runtime/Core/Contracts/IWorkflowSchedulerWorkQueue.cs#L23-L35)). The current distributed composition exposes [IExecutionCommandTransport](https://github.com/elsa-workflows/elsa-foundation/blob/b4d576e624ee8854822171dd3662ae02b4145727/Essentials/src/Workflows/Runtime/Distributed/Elsa.Workflows.Runtime.Distributed/Contracts/IExecutionCommandTransport.cs#L12-L29) as a durable cross-node inbox replacement contract; the EF implementation uses its frozen [v1 transport wire](https://github.com/elsa-workflows/elsa-foundation/blob/b4d576e624ee8854822171dd3662ae02b4145727/Essentials/src/Workflows/Runtime/Elsa.Workflows.Runtime/EXTENSION_POINTS.md#L573-L577). ADR 0078 and the actor-provider contract do not themselves require Orleans to reproduce this transport's exact lease/ack trace, but no current source authorizes removing or redefining the production transport contract. Before implementation, trace which existing durable record(s) carry each accepted command and how they are rediscovered/replayed; determine whether Orleans dispatch must use IExecutionCommandTransport or may add a transient direct route only after admission to those same durable records. Preserve the existing interface and frozen wire unless separately reviewed. Do not add another durable authority. Also resolve the generic actor mapping, supported Orleans/package/target-framework combination, membership source, host/client startup-shutdown/disposal, and transient reactivation behavior from supported APIs. The Orleans 10.4.0 process probe in #2900 is not an adopted version or production guarantee.
- The membership-provider classifier contains an example for `Extensions/Orleans/src/Cluster/Elsa.Cluster.Orleans.csproj` ([classifier](https://github.com/elsa-workflows/elsa-foundation/blob/b4d576e624ee8854822171dd3662ae02b4145727/Essentials/tests/Architecture/Tests/ArchitectureGuardTests.ClusterMembership.cs#L155-L159)); this is not a canonical project-path approval. The general path guard has no Orleans extension mapping ([path rules](https://github.com/elsa-workflows/elsa-foundation/blob/b4d576e624ee8854822171dd3662ae02b4145727/Essentials/tests/Architecture/Tests/ArchitectureGuardTests.cs#L550-L639)), and no implementation exists at that example path. Choose module names and extension layout together, then add narrow path/classifier mappings for actual membership and runtime adapter projects and tests. Preserve the refusal for unregistered roots and existing built-in/EF project locations.

Record the design mapping before implementation. If the membership or lifecycle requirements cannot be met without a second authority or a contract change, stop for an independently reviewed ADR decision first.

## Acceptance

1. Add an opt-in Orleans extension; absent its registration, built-in hosting remains selected. DI tests prove exactly one active membership and actor provider; duplicate/conflicting registration fails startup clearly. Prove owned Orleans and runtime resources are disposed on normal shutdown and startup failure. Register the chosen optional-extension project paths explicitly in the architecture guards and generated solution/filter graph; neither a classifier-only hint nor a blanket path exemption is sufficient.
2. Reuse #3056's shared native scenario helpers ([fixture](https://github.com/elsa-workflows/elsa-foundation/blob/b4d576e624ee8854822171dd3662ae02b4145727/Essentials/tests/Workbench/Tests/NativeHostingFixture.cs), [conformance tests](https://github.com/elsa-workflows/elsa-foundation/blob/b4d576e624ee8854822171dd3662ae02b4145727/Essentials/tests/Workbench/Tests/NativeHostingConformanceTests.cs)). Run profile-neutral behavior through both providers: unchanged published Sequence → Flowchart → held parallel background bodies → one native continuation, both receipt orders, stable invocation/attempt identities, cancellation and recovery. Keep provider-specific observations appropriately scoped.
3. With owned real processes and recorded distinct PIDs/host IDs, demonstrate owner loss/restart, recovery of accepted work after its transient route is lost, and exactly one logical continuation. Prove the command is idempotently durably admitted before routing and remains discoverable/replayable from the existing scheduler/outbox/transport record(s) that own the owed work. Retain/correlate receipts, membership/placement, and provider-specific routing, admission and acceptance evidence; do not require the built-in send/lease/ack trace shape where the reviewed design uses another transient route over those same durable records.
4. Prove equal execution IDs in different partitions do not collide. Exercise concurrent activations and send the original unchanged stale commit to EF under a live higher successor fence: require exact stale-token rejection, unchanged protected state and survivor recovery. Reuse the targeted EF-fence-removal negative control and retain its adverse result plus restored passing run.
5. Run existing provider/membership conformance at its owning layers: complete fresh reads and report visibility, capability filtering, incarnation/lapse/draining, duplicate registration and lost-route redelivery. Show that EF still owns commit fencing/checkpoints and that accepted owed work remains in the existing durable authority; Orleans storage/streams/mailboxes do not become its record of work. Retain the current IExecutionCommandTransport contract and frozen wire unless a separate reviewed change approves otherwise.
6. Preserve reproducible source, package/binary, run, receipt and process identities; commands, results, skips and limitations. Run applicable package/locked-restore/test, architecture, map and diff gates, exact-head review and post-merge CI/maps verification. Report skips as skips.

## Non-goals

No default-provider change, workflow/activity contract change, BPMN adapter, fourth model, unreviewed removal/replacement of the existing durable transport contract or wire, replacement of EF checkpoint fencing, multi-machine partition or performance claim, or exactly-once external-effect claim. Whether Orleans may use a transient direct route over the same durable records is a design question, not authorization for a second durable authority. External effects remain at least once.

**Sizing note for the program lead, not acceptance:** Keep this as one integration issue only if the design gate confirms supported Orleans membership and execution composition meet existing contracts. If proving those semantics requires a new contract/ADR, split that design resolution into a prerequisite issue and make adapter implementation plus same-suite qualification its dependent successor. Do not create or claim either before #3056 post-merge verification and a fresh queue review.

</details>

--- comments 6279709123 https://github.com/elsa-workflows/elsa-foundation/issues/2892#issuecomment-6279709123 2026-10-19T23:35:16Z ---

Read-only Orleans successor preparation — source gate, not implementation acceptance

#3056 / PR #3087 remains the sole active implementation leaf. This records bounded research while its hosted tests run; it does not create or claim a successor, select a package version, amend ADR 0078, or qualify Orleans. The earlier [prepared successor](https://github.com/elsa-workflows/elsa-foundation/issues/2892#issuecomment-6279240972) remains a draft.

Source baseline: official [Orleans v10.4.0 release](https://github.com/dotnet/orleans/releases/tag/v10.4.0), commit `d114d7522a53b8cce4c6b97d44293e5c7273d766`. Package/TFM and host topology still need confirmation before adoption. Root reviewed the downloaded pinned source and corrected the independent research's initial refresh overstatement.

* **Fresh reads need an explicit barrier.** `ClusterMembershipService.Refresh(default)` calls the membership manager without `requireFresh: true`. The manager's default path can share an already-running `pendingRefresh`, so a read started before publication may satisfy the public refresh. The internal fresh branch initiates a separate read, but that flag is not exposed by the public cluster-membership refresh. Do not equate a refreshed local snapshot with Elsa's required post-publication authoritative read. [Pinned service](https://github.com/dotnet/orleans/blob/d114d7522a53b8cce4c6b97d44293e5c7273d766/src/Orleans.Runtime/MembershipService/ClusterMembershipService.cs), [pinned manager](https://github.com/dotnet/orleans/blob/d114d7522a53b8cce4c6b97d44293e5c7273d766/src/Orleans.Runtime/MembershipService/MembershipTableManager.cs).
* **Table versions are not heartbeat/report revisions.** `IMembershipTable.ReadAllAsync` supplies an atomic complete member/table-version snapshot. Membership insert/update changes that version; heartbeat-only `UpdateIAmAliveAsync` preserves it. Standard membership entries have no Elsa report payload. A report barrier therefore cannot rely on heartbeat updates or an unchanged membership version. [Pinned table contract](https://github.com/dotnet/orleans/blob/d114d7522a53b8cce4c6b97d44293e5c7273d766/src/Orleans.Core/SystemTargetInterfaces/IMembershipTable.cs).
* **Cached silo metadata is insufficient for dynamic reports.** Orleans metadata is immutable per silo incarnation and read through a local background-updated cache. Elsa recomputes report sources on publication and requires every later Fresh read to observe the published report. Do not silently freeze those reports to fit metadata caching. [Official silo metadata](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/orleans/host/configuration-guide/silo-metadata), [Elsa contract at reviewed source](https://github.com/elsa-workflows/elsa-foundation/blob/b656e3a8ed8bc59d02291da4af3912fd16b7139b/Essentials/src/Cluster/Core/Contracts/IClusterMembership.cs).

Design proposal to evaluate, not an accepted mechanism: a custom membership-table/storage integration could couple versioned reports to the same authoritative store and a coherent combined snapshot. A companion report representation is not automatically a second membership authority; an independently decisive registry or uncoordinated join would not establish the existing contract. Specify atomicity, visibility, read/write failure, incarnation cleanup and provider maintenance costs, rather than assuming a turnkey Orleans facility. Preserve [ADR 0078](https://github.com/elsa-workflows/elsa-foundation/blob/b656e3a8ed8bc59d02291da4af3912fd16b7139b/docs/adr/0078-workflow-executions-are-virtual-actors-and-cluster-membership-is-a-foundation-contract.md).

The successor design gate must test: hold an old membership read open; commit a new report; start a Fresh read on another member; ensure it cannot return the old report. Also test failed writes, failed/partial reads, report-only updates with unchanged heartbeat/table-version assumptions, and conservative Dead/suspect/local-lapse mapping. An atomic membership snapshot does not prove a process declared Dead has stopped executing.

Root's current native-contract check adds two compatibility constraints: preserve `(partition, execution ID)` with collision-safe grain addressing, and do not serialize `WorkflowExecutionCommandDispatchOptions.AmbientServices` across a route. The current forwarding actor drops request-affine options and returns `Deferred` after durable transport admission; the Orleans mapping must preserve dispatch outcomes and durable owed-work authority, rather than equating a grain RPC acknowledgement with workflow success. [Actor models](https://github.com/elsa-workflows/elsa-foundation/blob/b656e3a8ed8bc59d02291da4af3912fd16b7139b/Essentials/src/Workflows/Runtime/Core/Models/WorkflowExecutionActorModels.cs), [forwarding behavior](https://github.com/elsa-workflows/elsa-foundation/blob/b656e3a8ed8bc59d02291da4af3912fd16b7139b/Essentials/src/Workflows/Runtime/Distributed/Elsa.Workflows.Runtime.Distributed/Services/ForwardingWorkflowExecutionActor.cs).

After #3056 is merged and verified, refresh the queue and decide whether this fits the adapter's existing design gate or requires a separately reviewed prerequisite ADR/spec. No contract relaxation or new implementation leaf is approved by this note.

--- comments 6281576654 https://github.com/elsa-workflows/elsa-foundation/issues/2892#issuecomment-6281576654 2026-10-20T02:00:27Z ---

Inactive Orleans successor: public-API and contract clarification

Read-only follow-up narrows the earlier Fresh-read uncertainty. Direct public `IMembershipTable.ReadAllAsync` is a credible candidate for fresh membership reads through the same selected clustering provider: Orleans exposes shared silo DI, providers register the table there, and the contract promises a complete atomic membership/table-version read. `ClusterMembershipService.Refresh` coalescing does not rule that route out. [Pinned public table contract](https://github.com/dotnet/orleans/blob/d114d7522a53b8cce4c6b97d44293e5c7273d766/src/Orleans.Core/SystemTargetInterfaces/IMembershipTable.cs#L117-L138), [provider registration](https://github.com/dotnet/orleans/blob/d114d7522a53b8cce4c6b97d44293e5c7273d766/src/AdoNet/Orleans.Clustering.AdoNet/AdoNetHostingExtensions.cs#L31-L45).

Dynamic report publication remains unresolved by those APIs. The inspected table has no report-write facet; heartbeat and startup-configured cached silo metadata are insufficient. A provider-owned report facet can be investigated, preserving one membership authority, post-publication revision visibility on later Fresh reads, complete-fleet-or-failure behavior, incarnation safety and the existing conservative capability-query rules.

Root and an independent contract audit also removed an unnecessary design constraint from the local successor draft: Foundation requires one semantic FleetView and the visibility guarantees above, **not a prescribed global membership-plus-report storage transaction**. A transaction or revision barrier is a candidate backend mechanism, not additional ratified acceptance. [Current Foundation contract](https://github.com/elsa-workflows/elsa-foundation/blob/f24cda73ea98345cd2686f8d3810b41b3fef6cf1/Essentials/src/Cluster/Core/Contracts/IClusterMembership.cs#L18-L58), [report revisions](https://github.com/elsa-workflows/elsa-foundation/blob/f24cda73ea98345cd2686f8d3810b41b3fef6cf1/Essentials/src/Cluster/Core/Models/PublishedMemberReport.cs#L3-L7).

The research pin is not a package/TFM/topology choice. All four successor design gates remain: membership/report semantics, collision-safe partition/execution identity, a shared-fixture provider composition seam, and explicit conflicting actor-provider selection. No successor leaf, claim or implementation is activated. #3056 / PR #3087 remains the sole implementation leaf; current-head Studio and maps passed, backend CI is running, and merge/post-merge verification remains open.

--- comments 6299076556 https://github.com/elsa-workflows/elsa-foundation/issues/2892#issuecomment-6299076556 2026-10-20T19:12:05Z ---

Owner intent recorded on 20 October 2026: discuss and design a durable, horizontally scalable task runner after delivery of the current unified execution program. This is a follow-up component, not an added acceptance criterion or active leaf for #2892.

Preserve a boundary between durable work acceptance and dispatch eligibility. The desired workflow pause behavior is to retain newly received work while withholding new starts for that workflow. Distinguish queue-wide pause from workflow-associated dispatch pause on a shared queue; define explicit behavior for already running work, cancellation, retries, leases, resume and recovery. Pause must not imply that in-flight external effects were undone or that workers have stopped.

An open design question is whether a narrow semantic adapter contract can support existing background worker frameworks as well as an out-of-the-box implementation. Evaluate capability differences before stabilizing that contract; do not create a universal queue abstraction prematurely or select a provider now. Reuse proven durable work identity, trusted scope, immutable invocation/result addresses and at-least-once effect constraints where the concrete integration fits. Keep actor placement and task execution as separate responsibilities. Current finite ETL work does not claim workflow/queue pause support.

The active #3109 / draft #3111 authoring API work continues. No Orleans work is resumed, no Proto.Actor selection is made, and no new task-runner implementation lane is opened.

Recovery creates exactly the second keyed start, parks it, completes both original catches/join and leaves later recipients unchanged. One execution exists per selected artifact; the later binding creates none.
